6b:["$","p",null,{"children":"The RMSE is the Root Mean Square Error between the solution and the reference solution (aggregated over the two functions for the Euler, and tensile test cases). For the Burgers, Euler, and tensile test cases, it is measured on the QPU over the sampled points. For the Black-Scholes cases, it is computed inside the domain, excluding the final time and the edges where the solution is imposed, on a fine grid (600 points per axis for 1 asset, 80 for 2 assets, 16 for 3 assets), by noiseless simulation at the parameters retained on the QPU. The classical compute time is the time spent by the optimizers on the classical side of the hybrid loop. It is mostly due to the evaluation of the points on the grid, which is easily parallelizable. The QPU time is the quantum processing time billed for the optimization jobs.","className":"group paragraph-el text-text-primary mx-0"}]
